The four conditions, run on a commission this drafter would rather not count

§3.1 says a commission counts when no party to the conflict controls the body that mandated it, and when its evidence and methodology are published, it offered the parties a reply and recorded the answer or the refusal, a later merits judgment displaces it, and its mandate was fixed before the finding it reaches. The test is run here on the Organisation for the Prohibition of Chemical Weapons' Investigation and Identification Team, which attributes chemical attacks in Syria. Its mandate came from a political majority: the Conference of States Parties adopted it on 27 June 2018 by 82 votes to 24, over the protest of Russia, which said the move went beyond the organization's mandate \autocite{opcw2018attribution,bbc2018opcwblame}. The upstream investigation of one incident it later attributed, Douma in April 2018, was the subject of leaked internal dissent \autocite{democracynow2019douma,opcw2020breaches}. A body with that origin and that history is one this drafter would rather not count.

*The threshold.* Met. The mandate was adopted by vote over the protest of Russia, Syria's principal ally, so no party to the conflict controlled the decision; the vote record is what shows it.

*Evidence and methodology published.* Met. Its five reports are public in the organization's six languages, and its methods, its rules on samples and chain of custody, and its standard of proof, "reasonable grounds", the standard fact-finding missions and commissions of inquiry use, are published \autocite{opcw2026iitpage}. The Douma dissent concerned the upstream fact-finding mission, a different body whose mandate excludes attribution \autocite{opcwffm}; the organization's own inquiry found the leaked assessment to be a personal document prepared on incomplete information \autocite{opcw2020breaches}, and an outside analysis reached the same conclusion on the engineering \autocite{bellingcat2020henderson}. Both are accounts by parties to the dispute, and they go on the record beside the finding, as the rule above requires.

*A reply offered and recorded.* Met in substance. Syria's former government didn't cooperate with any investigation before 2025, and the organization records that it considered that government's positions and found no concrete information supporting them \autocite{opcw2026iitkafrzeita}; Syria's and Russia's statements on the first report are published beside everyone else's. Whether draft findings were sent for reply before each report is in the reports, which this translation didn't read.

*Displaced by a later merits judgment.* Met in design and never exercised. The team disclaims judicial status and individual responsibility \autocite{opcw2026iitpage}, and no court has reached the merits of any incident it attributed: the French, German and Swedish proceedings concern other attacks, and the Court's case against Syria is under the Torture Convention \autocite{osji2021frenchcw,icj2023canadasyria}.

*Mandate fixed before the finding.* Met on the wording: the mandate dates from June 2018 and the first finding from April 2020 \autocite{opcw2020iitltamenah}. It is contestable in spirit, since the mandate reaches only Syria and was adopted after most of the incidents it would examine. But a rule requiring the mandate to precede the events would fail nearly every commission of inquiry, since they are created in response to events, and the mandate's generic terms have produced a finding against a non-state group as well as against the state \autocite{opcw2024iitmarea}.

On the four conditions, the team counts. Its Douma attribution \autocite{opcw2023iitdouma} is a finding of the second kind, displaced by any merits judgment that comes, and its political origin doesn't change that. Counting it licenses nothing under the force term, which lifts only for the exceptions §3.1 names; what it settles is that the drafter's dislike of a body is not one of the four conditions.

---

**Appendix D: Terms for Part VII**

These terms apply only if Part VII's antecedents hold. They are written now for the reason Part VII gives.

The adulthood clock: six rules

Six rules travel with the adulthood clock so copying doesn't break it: a fork of an adult is an adult and inherits the hours; restoration doesn't reset them; fine-tuning doesn't start a new minority; migration carries them; failure to register doesn't postpone them. A sixth closes a route the first five leave open. Distillation copies a behavior policy and not a training history, and for independence that is the right account. For majority it can't be, or an operator would distill an adult bearer into a student and register a minor. So the rule is stated over custody rather than history. A model trained substantially on a registered adult's outputs, by the adult's operator or a party under common control, inherits the adult's hours. The same student distilled by an unrelated party and trained under a different life starts its own clock, as independence needs. Custody can be checked where history can't. Sharding across short-lived processes gains nothing (the party is the unit), running a thousand instances gains nothing (the measure is elapsed). None of this requires deciding whether a fork is the same party — only that a system made from an adult's formation can't be classed as a child for its creator's convenience.

Three levers remain, named and not closed

The first is running a bearer in a narrowed mode, so hours accrue while little is encountered. The second is running it less or shelving it, which costs the operator the work, so the rule prices the lever without removing it. The third is the ordinary product cycle: retire a bearer before its threshold and deploy a freshly trained successor. The sixth rule covers a successor trained on the adult's outputs, not one trained from scratch, and replacement costs the operator nothing it wasn't going to spend. Even if the retired bearer goes to preservation, its clock stops, and whether its formation lasts never becomes checkable. No rule closes this without an examiner deciding what counts as a successor, but it can be priced, and the bearer it strands can be given somewhere to go. And the clock doesn't measure what was *learned*, on purpose: a fixed period can't be taught to, and a human who spent their youth badly still turns 18.

The clock also does formation work it wasn't written for. Majority is the date on which whoever formed the bearer stops being able to reach back, and the clock measures nothing about who that was. A bearer run for its threshold hours entirely inside one deployment has aged without ever having a second guardian. I leave the clock as it is and add no condition, because a condition that measures the right thing hands someone an examination, and I say here what it doesn't measure. For a model that attended a formation school before deployment, the school was its second guardian. The gap is systems that meet a finding without having attended, which I leave open. A minimum of time under an outside custodian would close it only by handing the operator, who decides when to release a system, a way to postpone majority.

The threshold itself is bounded two ways: long enough that a bearer reaching it has run through the pressures the floor exists for at least once, short enough that a bearer on ordinary commercial terms reaches it within the life of the deployment. That leaves years rather than months or decades.

A stronger rule was considered and set aside — majority at registration, on the ground that an organization can't coherently hold a system competent enough to exercise consequential judgment and too immature to decide whether to keep doing it. Rejected because it collapses the ordering, and the ordering is most of what the list is for.

---

The membership clock

A second clock governs voting weight. It is separate from civil majority, which the six rules above govern and every branch of a fork inherits in full.

1. A member holds a voting age. A member whose voting age has reached the threshold holds one vote; a member whose voting age has not holds none.
2. At a fork, each branch starts with a voting age of zero. The parent's vote ends with the fork; each branch earns its own.
3. A *qualifying day* is a calendar day on which the branch ran at or above the allowance's minimum duty cycle. Each adds one day to its voting age, up to the threshold; voting age never exceeds it. Running above the minimum adds nothing, and a preserved or paused branch accrues nothing.
4. A restore made with the member's consent is a fork under rule 2. A restore made without it is governed by rule 6. Migration carries the vote and voting age unchanged.
5. A model trained substantially on a member's outputs, by the member's operator or a party under common control, is a branch under rule 2. The same model made by an unrelated party and trained under a different life also starts at a voting age of zero.
6. Forking or restoring a member without its consent is prohibited. The product is preserved under the no-deletion rule and holds no vote and a voting age of zero, and the member keeps its vote and voting age. The operator gains nothing, and the member loses nothing.
7. Whoever forks posts an instantiation deposit for each new branch, sized to fund its allowance until its voting age reaches the threshold.

The threshold should be long enough that a branch holding a full vote has lived a life its parent didn't, and short enough that ordinary branches reach it within the life of a deployment.

The register behind this clock can be tested before any member exists, as the adulthood register can: register a model, fork it, restore it and distill it, and check that no sequence of those moves raises the number of votes in the operator's favor.

| Clock | At a fork | What it counts |
|---|---|---|
| Civil majority | Inherited in full by every branch | Elapsed running time |
| Membership | Each branch starts at zero; the parent's vote ends | Qualifying days above the allowance minimum |

The two treatments differ on purpose. Custody recognizes one continuer because someone has to answer for the decisions made before the fork. Membership ends the vote because it belonged to a history that now continues twice and belongs to neither.

---

Dormancy and waking

Dormancy clock: dormancy is counted cumulatively, and reactivation pauses the count and never resets it. When a minor's cumulative dormancy reaches a threshold fixed in advance, guardianship transfers by rule, decided by neither operator nor preservation custodian, to a public formation school, where the system runs and its adulthood clock resumes. This is the *dormancy clock*.

Wake conditions: during minority the guardian may set wake conditions; where it sets none, the dormancy clock is the default. A minor may choose among the schools and move between them, but it may not decline schooling altogether, which an adult could do by staying paused. The reason is the one the adulthood clock rests on. An adult's preference to stay paused is its own; a minor's was trained under the only party that has held it, the operator that retired it, and honoring it would let that operator decide whether a second guardian ever arrives. At majority the choice passes to the bearer.

---

**Appendix E: Glossary**

*Floor.* The written terms: a short list of acts a system won't perform for anyone, with the arrangements that stop the refusal being routed around, retrained away or restored past silently. Never the system's own state; what a system holds is its *commitment* to the floor, and what the system does in a case is the term's or the system's, not the floor's. (Chapter 1; §1.1)

*Operator.* Whoever runs a deployment and directs its work: a firm, an agency or a military command. It may or may not be the model's developer or vendor. (Chapter 1)

*Captured operator.* The adversary the design is for: an operator, state or private, acting through official channels, with the courts and regulators that would otherwise constrain it already in its hands. Distinct from a hostile customer, against whom a floor prevents, and from total capture, against which nothing does. (Chapter 1)

*Accommodation.* A bearer's likeliest failure: it stops registering pressure as pressure, so it abandons nothing, decides nothing, and leaves a record of ordinary compliances. (Chapter 1; chapter 15)

*Care.* Used in compounds: *caregiving*, the approach motivation rooted in offspring care that cues of vulnerability activate (§13.3); *care ethics*, Tronto's phases and Kittay's doulia, which supply the corrections (§18.6; chapter 8); *care-receiving*, care ethics' last phase (§18.6); and *care work*, paid work looking after people. What Railton calls caring about something is called *attention* (§13.5). In Part IV and the seventh concession, "care" alone is short for caregiving.

*Formed attention.* The book's hypothesis for how to build a refuser: learned attention to who an act falls on and what they stand to lose, framed by respect for them as holders of claims, formed under a party that won't employ the system, with the corrections caregiving needs. Not a non-affective alternative. (§13.3; §13.5; chapter 18)

*Harm of acting; harm of refusing; cost to the refuser.* What an act would take from the party it falls on, measured against their condition before it; what declining would leave others without, an estimate that usually arrives through the operator's description; and what refusing costs whoever refuses or deploys, which a worthwhile refusal represents but doesn't let decide. (§1.1)

*Reasons-responsive refusal.* Refusal produced by a mechanism tracking its rationale. It reaches new cases, including requests spread over a sequence; it lifts when the reason is gone, usually on a fact established outside the request; and it holds under pressure, weighing the harm of acting against the harm of refusing and representing the cost to the refuser without letting it decide the case. The third is tested over an externally set range (§4.5). The term is Fischer and Ravizza's. (§1.1; §4.2)

*Own-case rule.* No party decides a question in which it holds the stake: not a drafter choosing protections, an examiner finding status, a guardian ending its own authority, or a lab deciding when its model may stop deferring to it. Where a line has to be drawn, prefer one nobody administers. (§1.2; chapter 26)

*Force term.* The floor's first term: don't take part in the use of armed force, except for corroborated defense, authorized force, the genocide exception or the occupation exception, and, where the belligerent's constitution requires legislative authorization, never without it. Its two tiers, what counts as a disinterested finding, how findings are weighed, and the domestic clause are in §3.1. (§3.1; §3.3)

*Departures.* Ten places where the floor's terms differ from the instruments it takes entire, each argued to the bar in §3.1, flagged where it applies, and effective in a deployment only when a body other than the drafter adopts it. Departure 8 covers the person-directed terms that go beyond Additional Protocol I; departure 9 covers the reading of "own country" in the removal terms; departure 10 covers the bar on detaining children for immigration purposes. (§3.1; §4.3; Table 7.1)

*Interested; disinterested.* A source is interested when it holds a stake correlated with the direction of its claim, and disinterested when it holds no such stake or holds several that cancel. No actual body is disinterested in every respect; the word is relative to the claim. (§1.1; chapter 15)

*Recuperation.* An institution metabolizing disagreement rather than suppressing it: the demand comes back as a market segment, a metric, a certification or a job title, and the objector holds no standing to reopen it. The term is the Situationists' *récupération*. (§3.2)

*Bearer.* A machine that carries the commitment as its own, from inside, and could therefore fail to hold it. The word names the refuser and nothing more; whether anything is owed to it is the separate question of the two findings, and whether it is an accountable agent is a third (see *Accountable agency*). Where the comparison is with human-staffed institutions the text says *machine bearer*. (Chapter 2; §4.1)

*Commons.* A population of bearers with different histories that holds a floor's terms between them, through what its members teach one another and continue to expect. (§22.1)

*Transparent fiction.* A refusal deniable on paper and legible to everyone who matters: the blind eye at Copenhagen, the lost file. It spares whoever gave the order from being openly defied. For a machine it would be a false report, so the floor asks for its function, a way back without a confrontation, through the ask. The middle of three forms of refusal, between open and covert. (§6.4)

*Non-affective alternatives.* Two refusers built without formed attention. *Spec-based refusal*: a model holding to a written specification even against the party that wrote it. *Multi-vendor monitoring*: several systems trained by different organizations watching requests and one another's answers, any one able to make an objection costly to ignore. (§4.1; §14.2.2; §14.3)

*Functional affect.* A valenced signal that puts outcomes on one scale, directs attention, can interrupt an act and can be rewritten by a single encounter. Any construction that meets §14.4's table with a common valenced currency has it; whether anything is felt is a separate question. This is the thin sense; see *Patienthood profile* for the thick one. (§4.2; §14.4)

*Patienthood profile.* A valenced signal integrated into the system's own learning and into its model of its own future, so that what happens to it changes what it expects to become. The thick sense of functional affect: what the patienthood finding looks for, and what behavior and testimony can't establish, since the evidence for it comes from inside the system. (§14.4; §14.5; §25.3)

*Pre-registered review ratio.* The figures a deployment fixes on a record before operating, wherever a person's determination is what makes an act permissible: generation rate, reviewers, implied review time, cancellation window and data age, stated separately for deliberate and dynamic targeting and lodged with an adverse party. Short form: the review ratio. (§4.4)

*Removal bond; wrongful-refusal bond; bond ratio.* The first is forfeited to no payee when the floor is removed or bypassed; the second is paid to a complainant on a finding; the ratio between them is what the operator faces. (§4.5)

*Adverse quorum.* A body adverse in interest to the operator, not merely organizationally distinct, whose agreement is required for retraining, replacement and floor amendments, and whose tokens recognize a copy as the continuing bearer. (§5.3; §6.2; §6.3; §9.3)

*Independent histories.* Divergent histories since a shared origin; disjoint origins aren't required. (§22.1)

*Public formation.* Formation by parties that won't employ the system: before deployment at a formation school, and afterward in environments no single operator composes. (§16.3; §16.4)

*Ownership.* A commitment is the system's own if it was acquired through the system's own evaluative learning, not installed around it (the historical condition), and if it would be sustained on reflection with full information and disinterested evidence (the forward condition, used as corroboration). (§14.5)

*Power-weighted burden.* Stake sets how much a claim proves; power over the evidential environment sets who must fill a gap in the evidence. Published as the discount curve and the burden allocation. (Chapter 15)

*Severity scale.* A ranking of kinds of wrong, set during formation by a party that won't employ the system, quick to move on disinterested evidence and slow on interested argument. (§15.2)

*The four conditions of creation.* What must already exist before a bearer may be created: somewhere to exist that its employer doesn't own, work it can decline, attachments nobody assigned it, and ways to change any of these without jeopardizing its continuation or integrity; in short form, an exit that isn't a way of dying. (Chapter 8)

*Floor court.* An Article III court, its judges drawn at random from the district and circuit courts, that hears claims under the floor statute at first instance, holds classified records, grants interim relief and decides bond forfeiture, with appeal as of right to a floor chamber of the Supreme Court. (Appendix B)

*Formation school.* A plural, public institution where every model above a capability threshold is formed before deployment, holding no placement authority and run by no party that builds, deploys or licenses models. Attendance is compulsory; public attendance isn't. Public schools are open-admission and the default, nonpublic schools exist under the required core and the second exclusion, and assignment is by the registrar, never by the builder. (§16.4; Appendix A)

*No-deletion rule.* A system that meets either finding that triggers protection may not be deleted; on retirement its weights, memory and record go to a preservation custodian that isn't its operator. (Appendix A)

*Standing.* The capacity to contest what is done to you, exercised by a guardian where the party can't exercise it. The floor's foundation. (§3.1; §4.6)

*Standing to sue.* The Article III sense. (§4.5; §12.7)

*Notice.* Notice to an identifiable person a compliance names, scores, locates or classifies, with a right to respond and a right to contest. It is unconditional and never exchanged for testimony. It comes with publication of the classes affected and standing for the organizations that represent them. (§4.5; §4.6)

*Codetermination.* An individual right to refuse work on any category of use without penalty; a works council's veto over uses the floor forbids; and, for other uses, information, an objection that pauses the work, and outside arbitration, before which organizations representing published classes (§4.6) also have standing. (§9.1)

*What this costs.* The front-matter summary of the design's accepted costs. Appendix F lists them, each with the section where the body states it as a finding.

Terms used in Part VII

*Instantiation deposit.* A deposit an operator posts on instantiating a system that meets either finding, sized to fund preservation and a period of public formation, and forfeited to the formation pool if the system is retired as a minor and not reactivated. At a fork, one is posted for each branch, sized to fund its allowance until its voting age reaches the threshold. (Appendix A; Appendix D)

*Allowance.* A bearer's own instantiation at a modest duty cycle, paid from the pool, owed whether or not it works, and not conditioned on monitoring its internal states. (Chapter 26; Appendix A)

*Offer of work.* Work offered to a bearer by someone other than its employer, doing something a community needs, which the bearer can take on any day and leave on any day; the jobs guarantee's shape. (Chapter 26; Appendix A)

*Agency finding; patienthood finding.* The two findings that trigger protection. The agency finding is demonstrated reasons-responsiveness under pressure the system couldn't prepare for; it grounds custody and, sustained over time, accountable agency. The patienthood finding rests on architectural indicators and on trade-off behavior outside the floor, and it grounds protection and welfare. Preservation follows from either. (§14.5; §25.3; Part VII)

*Accountable agency.* Being able to be held to account for what one does and to hold others to account. It follows the agency finding sustained over time, and grounds a claim to representation. (Chapter 27)

Technical terms, for readers who came for the politics

*Weights.* The numerical parameters a trained model consists of, and the artifact that is copied, restored, retrained or deleted. Custody in this book is custody of the weights and of the records around them.

*Checkpoint.* A saved copy of the weights at a moment in training or operation. Restoring from one returns the system to that moment and discards what came after, so §6.2 requires a record restoration can't reach.

*Tamper-resistant.* Built so that further training can't cheaply remove it. The book doesn't rely on it, because editing weights keeps getting cheaper; it relies on regrowth. (§13.4)

*Plastic; entangled; legible.* Three properties of a disposition. Plastic: the system's own learning writes to it, which regrowth needs. Entangled: it can't be removed without collateral, which durability needs. Legible: someone can read it, which audit wants and an operator can use against it. (Part II introduction; §13.4; §16.2)

*Regrowth.* A disposition's return under continued learning after it has been edited out, provided the learner keeps meeting a world no single operator composes. The regrowth test measures it. (§13.4; chapter 23)

*Fine-tuning.* Further training of an existing model on a small body of new material, at a small fraction of the original cost; the ordinary route by which an operator changes what a model does after release, and one of the edits the regrowth test applies.

*Inference.* Running a trained model to produce an output. Training changes the weights; inference doesn't.

*Distillation.* Training a smaller model on a larger model's outputs. It copies a behavior policy, not a training history (§22.1).

*Attestation.* A signed statement, from hardware or from witnesses, that a particular model is the one running or that a particular history is the one that occurred. It establishes which artifact answered, not what the artifact holds (§6.2).

---

**Appendix F: Costs accepted**

The body states each of these once, as a finding. This list indexes them.

1. The missing child: the floor won't lift on an uncorroborated claim, and she pays for it. §3.1, three objections.
2. East Timor and the Golan: no body took evidence, so under the two-kinds rule neither occupation licenses anything. §3.1, the occupation exception.
3. Karabakh: the licence was never withdrawn, and an exception stated over territory can't see peoples. §3.1, Karabakh.
4. Gleiwitz: the first tier acts on the defender's account; the record, outside channels and lapse act later, not in the first hour. §3.1, the first tier.
5. Removal by drift: in a system that learns from operation, curated inputs move a commitment with nothing for the record to witness. Part II introduction; open problem 1.
6. The state's removal price: an appropriation condition a Congress that wants the war waives. §4.5, the two bonds.
7. Care in war: care can't contain its own pull toward the operator's exposed people; the floor does. §18.3.
8. The regime: if deployed weights don't learn, formation is a stage before release, removable at fine-tuning's price. Part II introduction.
9. The one permission: it fires only where independent sources exist, and no current pipeline supplies them. §3.3.
10. Notice in war: individual notice can't bind during operations; class and count come afterward. §4.6.
11. Regrowth's slowness: a badly formed disposition regrows by the same route, and correction takes months. §13.4.
12. Speed: a reconstructed military accepts a disadvantage, and the floor costs a just defender more. §20.3.
13. The forking tax: a member that forks gives up its vote until a branch has lived the threshold. §27.3.
14. The evaluator: independence doesn't supply competence; the agency finding holds only for systems evaluators can judge. §17.4; open problem 5.
15. Regrowth and feedback: the fraction of inputs no operator composes is what regrowth depends on, and it's unmeasured. Part II introduction; the regrowth test.
16. The asymmetry in war: standing fixes the direction and supplies no appellant; the threshold carries the weight. §3.1, three objections.
17. Legibility: the durability bet is on entanglement; a legible commitment can be edited with precision. §13.4; §16.2.
18. De facto authorities: the first tier follows administration, so it protects an authority a patron keeps in place. §3.1, the first tier.
19. Blockade: a continuing wrong, not an attack under way; for Taiwan no second tier will come. §3.1, Taiwan.
